People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I was trying to tell myself it wasn’t affecting things that much, but it kind of is.

Work has definitely taken a hit. I’ve missed some buying deadlines, and a couple vendor relationships got… strained, I guess. I also made some purchasing calls too fast, without really thinking them through, and my supervisor gave me a formal warning for it.

And outside work, I’ve been pulling back from people. I stopped going to gallery nights and volleyball weekends with my friends, which I used to actually like. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it’s interfering.

I thought maybe it’d be better to talk to someone before I make it worse or overreact.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “plotting” sounds kind of intense, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m accusing everybody. But yeah, I can’t really shake this feeling that people at work—and even some vendors—are tracking what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nMy supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that’s happening, and I understand that, but the feeling still sticks around. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is something that's been going on for a long time, or is it a relatively new concern for you?\n\nPatient: It’s… not brand new, I guess. I didn’t really want to make a big deal out of it, but it’s been maybe around ten months or so that I’ve had these suspicions.\n\nAnd, um… the voice thing has been around about that long too. I know that probably sounds concerning, but I’m trying to answer it honestly.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a 'voice thing' that's been going on for about ten months, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it’s not like constant or anything. It’s just sometimes, like when I’m in the stockroom by myself, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me.\n\nIt’ll say things like, “Move faster, Scott,” or kind of laugh at what I picked… like it’s making fun of me. And then I turn around, or look, and there’s nobody there.\n\nI know that sounds weird. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just… that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel like you have to do?\n\nPatient: Not really like… big commands, I don’t think. It’s more like it’ll say, “Move faster, Scott,” like it’s criticizing me or pushing me, especially if I’m in the stockroom. Or it’ll laugh at what I picked.\n\nI mean, I do feel tense when it happens, like I should hurry up, but I wouldn’t say it’s making me do something dangerous or anything. And when I turn around, there’s nobody there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would hurt yourself or others?\n\nPatient: No… no, it hasn’t said anything like that. It’s more just… criticizing me, I guess. Like telling me to move faster, or laughing at what I’m doing.\n\nI know that still sounds bad, but it’s not telling me to hurt myself or anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about people tracking you and the voice you hear seem to be all part of one big thing to you, or are they separate things?\n\nPatient: I guess they feel connected, yeah… not like I have it all mapped out or anything. I don’t want to sound paranoid.\n\nBut what really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. And my supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that, so I know maybe I’m overreading it… but it still feels like it’s happening.\n\nThe voice feels kind of tied into that, like criticizing me or making me feel watched, if that makes sense. I might jump around a little because I’m nervous, but it feels like it follows a line to me. Is that… does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and feelings affecting your ability to do your job or take care of daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I was trying to tell myself it wasn’t affecting things that much, but it kind of is.\n\nWork has definitely taken a hit. I stopped going to gallery nights and volleyball weekends with my friends, which I used to actually like. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it’s interfering.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that hearing a voice criticizing you is a normal experience?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know it’s probably not, like, a normal thing most people have. It’s just sometimes, like when I’m in the stockroom by myself, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me.\n\nIt’ll say things like, “Move faster, Scott,” or kind of laugh at what I picked… like it’s making fun of me.   },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole “scheme,” because that sounds kind of intense. But yeah, in my head it does feel connected.

What really made me come in is I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and maybe even some vendors, are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I end up looking stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me reports and they don’t prove that’s happening, I still keep feeling like there’s something going on behind it.

I know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to be difficult or accuse everybody… it’s just hard to turn off.

And then I turn around, or look, and there’s nobody there.\n\nI know that sounds weird. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just… that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel like you have to do?\n\nPatient: Not really like… big commands, I don’t think. Like telling me to move faster, or laughing at what I’m doing.\n\nI know that still sounds bad, but it’s not telling me to hurt myself or anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about people tracking you and the voice you hear seem to be all part of one big thing to you, or are they separate things?\n\nPatient: I guess they feel connected, yeah… not like I have it all mapped out or anything. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is something that's been going on for a long time, or is it a relatively new concern for you?\n\nPatient: It’s… not brand new, I guess. I didn’t really want to make a big deal out of it, but it’s been maybe around ten months or so that I’ve had these suspicions.\n\nAnd, um… the voice thing has been around about that long too. I know that probably sounds concerning, but I’m trying to answer it honestly.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a 'voice thing' that's been going on for about ten months, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it’s not like constant or anything. It’s just sometimes, like when I’m in the stockroom by myself, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me.\n\nIt’ll say things like, “Move faster, Scott,” or kind of laugh at what I picked… like it’s making fun of me. And then I turn around, or look, and there’s nobody there.\n\nI know that sounds weird. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just… that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel like you have to do?\n\nPatient: Not really like… big commands, I don’t think. It’s more like it’ll say, “Move faster, Scott,” like it’s criticizing me or pushing me, especially if I’m in the stockroom. Or it’ll laugh at what I picked.\n\nI mean, I do feel tense when it happens, like I should hurry up, but I wouldn’t say it’s making me do something dangerous or anything. And when I turn around, there’s nobody there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would hurt yourself or others?\n\nPatient: No… no, it hasn’t said anything like that. It’s more just… criticizing me, I guess. Like telling me to move faster, or laughing at what I’m doing.\n\nI know that still sounds bad, but it’s not telling me to hurt myself or anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about people tracking you and the voice you hear seem to be all part of one big thing to you, or are they separate things?\n\nPatient: I guess they feel connected, yeah… not like I have it all mapped out or anything. I don’t want to sound paranoid.\n\nBut what really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. And my supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that, so I know maybe I’m overreading it… but it still feels like it’s happening.\n\nThe voice feels kind of tied into that, like criticizing me or making me feel watched, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense and follow a logical order?\n\nPatient: Um… I think they make sense to me, mostly. Like, in my head there’s a reason I’m thinking each thing, even if I’m not explaining it perfectly.\n\nI don’t know if it comes out jumbled when I talk. I hope not. I might jump around a little because I’m nervous, but it feels like it follows a line to me. Is that… does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and feelings affecting your ability to do your job or take care of daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I was trying to tell myself it wasn’t affecting things that much, but it kind of is.\n\nWork has definitely taken a hit. I’ve missed some buying deadlines, and a couple vendor relationships got… strained, I guess. I also made some purchasing calls too fast, without really thinking them through, and my supervisor gave me a formal warning for it.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve been pulling back from people. I stopped going to gallery nights and volleyball weekends with my friends, which I used to actually like. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it’s interfering.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that hearing a voice criticizing you is a normal experience?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know it’s probably not, like, a normal thing most people have. And then I turn around and nobody’s there. So… yeah, I guess I know that’s not normal. My supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that’s happening, and I get that… but the feeling doesn’t go away.\n\nAnd then when I hear the voice criticizing me, it sort of lines up with that, like it’s part of the same thing. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it’s interfering.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that hearing a voice criticizing you is a normal experience?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know it’s probably not, like, a normal thing most people have. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m… pretty convinced, I guess. Not 100%, because I know that sounds extreme and I don’t want to accuse people unfairly.

But what really made me come in is that I can’t shake it — the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me reports that don’t prove that, it still feels like that’s what’s happening.

So maybe… like, more convinced than I want to be? Does that make sense?

I don’t want to sound paranoid.\n\nBut what really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. And my supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that, so I know maybe I’m overreading it… but it still feels like it’s happening.\n\nThe voice feels kind of tied into that, like criticizing me or making me feel watched, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense and follow a logical order?\n\nPatient: Um… I think they make sense to me, mostly. Like, in my head there’s a reason I’m thinking each thing, even if I’m not explaining it perfectly.\n\nI don’t know if it comes out jumbled when I talk. I hope not. I might jump around a little because I’m nervous, but it feels like it follows a line to me. Is that… does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and feelings affecting your ability to do your job or take care of daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I was trying to tell myself it wasn’t affecting things that much, but it kind of is.\n\nWork has definitely taken a hit. I’ve missed some buying deadlines, and a couple vendor relationships got… strained, I guess. I also made some purchasing calls too fast, without really thinking them through, and my supervisor gave me a formal warning for it.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve been pulling back from people. I stopped going to gallery nights and volleyball weekends with my friends, which I used to actually like. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it’s interfering.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that hearing a voice criticizing you is a normal experience?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know it’s probably not, like, a normal thing most people have. I just feel embarrassed saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts about people tracking you and the voice criticizing you fit together in a way that makes sense to you, like a story or a theory?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess they kind of do fit together in my head, even if I know it probably sounds bad when I say it out loud.\n\nWhat really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and maybe even some vendors, are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I’ll look stupid. My supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that’s happening, and I get that… but the feeling doesn’t go away.\n\nAnd then when I hear the voice criticizing me, it sort of lines up with that, like it’s part of the same thing. I’m not saying I’m totally sure, but it feels connected. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a single explanation or reason behind everything that's been happening to you, including the voice and the feelings of being tracked?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, there kind of is one explanation my mind keeps going back to.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m accusing everyone, but what really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and maybe even some vendors — are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I’ll look stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me the reports and they don’t really prove that, it still feels like that’s what’s happening.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound great. I’m trying to be honest, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the people tracking you and the voice you hear are part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole “scheme,” because that sounds kind of intense. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but sometimes when I’m in the stockroom, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear a man’s voice like it’s right behind me.\n\nIt’ll say things like, “Move faster, Scott,” or kind of laugh at what I picked. And then I turn around and nobody’s there. So… yeah, I guess I know that’s not normal. I just feel embarrassed saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts about people tracking you and the voice criticizing you fit together in a way that makes sense to you, like a story or a theory?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess they kind of do fit together in my head, even if I know it probably sounds bad when I say it out loud.\n\nWhat really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and maybe even some vendors, are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I’ll look stupid. My supervisor has shown me reports that don’t really prove that’s happening, and I get that… but the feeling doesn’t go away.\n\nAnd then when I hear the voice criticizing me, it sort of lines up with that, like it’s part of the same thing. I’m not saying I’m totally sure, but it feels connected. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a single explanation or reason behind everything that's been happening to you, including the voice and the feelings of being tracked?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, there kind of is one explanation my mind keeps going back to.\n\nI don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m accusing everyone, but what really made me come in is I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and maybe even some vendors — are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I’ll look stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me the reports and they don’t really prove that, it still feels like that’s what’s happening.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound great. I’m trying to be honest, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the people tracking you and the voice you hear are part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole “scheme,” because that sounds kind of intense. But yeah, in my head it does feel connected.\n\nWhat really made me come in is I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and maybe even some vendors, are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I end up looking stupid. Even when my supervisor shows me reports and they don’t prove that’s happening, I still keep feeling like there’s something going on behind it.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to be difficult or accuse everybody… it’s just hard to turn off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice and the people tracking you are working together towards a specific goal?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say they’re definitely working together, like… I know that sounds like a lot. That’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things that are happening to you, like the voice and people tracking you, fit together in a way that makes sense, like a story or a theory?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess my brain does make it into a kind of story, even if I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nIt’s like I keep coming back to this idea that people at work, and maybe even vendors, are watching what I’m buying and then messing with the launch numbers so I’ll look stupid. It just… fits together that way in my head, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the people tracking you and messing with the launch numbers might not actually be doing that, or is that something you're completely certain about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… I know there’s a chance I’m wrong. Even when my supervisor shows me reports that don’t prove it, my mind still keeps going back there.\n\nSo… I guess I can admit it might not be happening, but it doesn’t feel that way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever change your mind about people tracking you and messing with the launch numbers?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to think evidence could change my mind.
